=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a 50 x 50 chessboard with one knight and some pawns on it. You are given two integers kx and ky where (kx, ky) denotes the position of the knight, and a 2D array positions where positions[i] = [x_i, y_i] denotes the position of the pawns on the chessboard.
Alice and Bob play a turn-based game, where Alice goes first. In each player's turn:

The player selects a pawn that still exists on the board and captures it with the knight in the fewest possible moves. Note that the player can select any pawn, it might not be one that can be captured in the least number of moves.
In the process of capturing the selected pawn, the knight may pass other pawns without capturing them. Only the selected pawn can be captured in this turn.

Alice is trying to maximize the sum of the number of moves made by both players until there are no more pawns on the board, whereas Bob tries to minimize them.
Return the maximum total number of moves made during the game that Alice can achieve, assuming both players play optimally.
Note that in one move, a chess knight has eight possible positions it can move to, as illustrated below. Each move is two cells in a cardinal direction, then one cell in an orthogonal direction.

 
Example 1:

Input: kx = 1, ky = 1, positions = [[0,0]]
Output: 4
Explanation:

The knight takes 4 moves to reach the pawn at (0, 0).

Example 2:

Input: kx = 0, ky = 2, positions = [[1,1],[2,2],[3,3]]
Output: 8
Explanation:


Alice picks the pawn at (2, 2) and captures it in two moves: (0, 2) -> (1, 4) -> (2, 2).
Bob picks the pawn at (3, 3) and captures it in two moves: (2, 2) -> (4, 1) -> (3, 3).
Alice picks the pawn at (1, 1) and captures it in four moves: (3, 3) -> (4, 1) -> (2, 2) -> (0, 3) -> (1, 1).


Example 3:

Input: kx = 0, ky = 0, positions = [[1,2],[2,4]]
Output: 3
Explanation:

Alice picks the pawn at (2, 4) and captures it in two moves: (0, 0) -> (1, 2) -> (2, 4). Note that the pawn at (1, 2) is not captured.
Bob picks the pawn at (1, 2) and captures it in one move: (2, 4) -> (1, 2).


 
Constraints:

0 <= kx, ky <= 49
1 <= positions.length <= 15
positions[i].length == 2
0 <= positions[i][0], positions[i][1] <= 49
All positions[i] are unique.
The input is generated such that positions[i] != [kx, ky] for all 0 <= i < positions.length.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.


A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Pseudo:

from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def maxMoves(self, kx: int, ky: int, positions: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(positions)
        # nodes 0..n-1 pawns, n knight initial
        points = positions + [[kx, ky]]
        # compute dist matrix
        moves = [(1,2),(1,-2),(-1,2),(-1,-2),(2,1),(2,-1),(-2,1),(-2,-1)]
        def bfs(sx, sy):
            dist = [[-1]*50 for _ in range(50)]
            q=deque([(sx,sy)])
            dist[sx][sy]=0
            while q:
                x,y=q.popleft()
                for dx,dy in moves:
                    nx,ny=x+dx,y+dy
                    if 0<=nx<50 and 0<=ny<50 and dist[nx][ny]==-1:
                        dist[nx][ny]=dist[x][y]+1
                        q.append((nx,ny))
            return dist
        # Could BFS from each unique point. There may be duplicate? positions unique and not k, so 16 unique. 
        dist = [[0]*(n+1) for _ in range(n+1)]
        for i,(x,y) in enumerate(points):
            d = bfs(x,y)
            for j,(px,py) in enumerate(points):
                dist[i][j] = d[px][py]
        # dp[mask][last] last in 0..n (n initial)
        size = 1<<n
        dp = [[0]*(n+1) for _ in range(size)]
        # iterate mask from 1 to size-1
        for mask in range(1, size):
            remaining = mask.bit_count()
            captured = n - remaining
            alice_turn = (captured % 2 == 0)
            # maybe precompute bits list
            bits = [i for i in range(n) if mask & (1<<i)]
            # For each last not in mask? Actually if last in mask invalid. But dp[mask][last] for valid last not in mask.
            for last in range(n+1):
                if last < n and (mask & (1<<last)):
                    continue
                # compute best
                if alice_turn:
                    best = -10**9
                    for i in bits:
                        val = dist[last][i] + dp[mask ^ (1<<i)][i]
                        if val > best: best = val
                else:
                    best = 10**9
                    for i in bits:
                        val = dist[last][i] + dp[mask ^ (1<<i)][i]
                        if val < best: best = val
                dp[mask][last] = best
        full = size - 1
        return dp[full][n]

Let's maybe write robust code:
```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def maxMoves(self, kx: int, ky: int, positions: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(positions)
        points = positions + [[kx, ky]]
        N = n + 1
        knight_moves = [(1,2),(1,-2),(-1,2),(-1,-2),(2,1),(2,-1),(-2,1),(-2,-1)]
        
        def bfs(sx, sy):
            d = [[-1]*50 for _ in range(50)]
            q = deque([(sx, sy)])
            d[sx][sy] = 0
            while q:
                x, y = q.popleft()
                for dx, dy in knight_moves:
                    nx, ny = x+dx, y+dy
                    if 0 <= nx < 50 and 0 <= ny < 50 and d[nx][ny] == -1:
                        d[nx][ny] = d[x][y] + 1
                        q.append((nx, ny))
            return d
        
        dist = [[0]*N for _ in range(N)]
        for i, (x, y) in enumerate(points):
            d = bfs(x, y)
            for j, (px, py) in enumerate(points):
                dist[i][j] = d[px][py]
        
        size = 1 << n
        dp = [[0]*N for _ in range(size)]
        # precompute bits for each mask? Could for speed.
        bits = [[] for _ in range(size)]
        for mask in range(size):
            # maybe list comprehension
            bits[mask] = [i for i in range(n) if mask & (1 << i)]
        # But storing lists for 32768 masks okay. Or compute inside.
```
Need think about memory for bits: 32768 lists, okay but unnecessary. Could precompute `popcount` and maybe `bits`? Simpler compute inside. But transitions ~2M, list comprehension for each mask and each last? If we compute bits inside mask loop once, okay. We can do:
```
for mask in range(1, size):
    rem = mask.bit_count()
    captured = n - rem
    alice = (captured & 1) == 0
    # iterate bits
    for last in range(N):
        if last < n and (mask & (1 << last)):
            continue
        if alice:
            best = -10**9
            m = mask
            while m:
                lb = m & -m
                i = lb.bit_length() - 1
                val = dist[last][i] + dp[mask ^ lb][i]
                if val > best: best = val
                m -= lb
        else:
            best = 10**9
            m = mask
            while m:
                lb = m & -m
                i = lb.bit_length() - 1
                val = dist[last][i] + dp[mask ^ lb][i]
                if val < best: best = val
                m -= lb
        dp[mask][last] = best
```

```
class Solution:
    def maxMoves(self, kx: int, ky: int, positions: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(positions)
        pos = positions + [[kx, ky]]
        dist = [[0]*(n+1) for _ in range(n+1)]

            ...

        def dfs(mask, last):
            if mask == 0: return 0
            turn = (n - mask.bit_count()) % 2
            if turn == 0:
                return max(dist[last][i] + dfs(mask ^ (1<<i), i) for i in range(n) if mask>>i & 1)
            else:
                return min(...)
```

Now, let's consider if we can further optimize by using `@lru_cache` recursion instead of iterative. Iterative avoids recursion limit? n=15 recursion depth 15, fine. But iterative code maybe more verbose. We can use recursive memo for clarity:
```
@lru_cache(None)
def dfs(mask, last):
    if mask == 0: return 0
    captured = n - mask.bit_count()
    vals = []
    for i in range(n):
       if mask & (1<<i):
          vals.append(dist[last][i] + dfs(mask ^ (1<<i), i))
    if captured % 2 == 0: return max(vals)
    else: return min(vals)
return dfs(full, n)
```
This is simpler and likely accepted. Recursion depth 15. `lru_cache` overhead for 524k states? Actually states valid ~? Number of (mask,last) valid = sum_{mask} (n+1-popcount(mask)) = (n+1)2^n - n2^{n-1} = 16*32768 - 15*16384 = 524288 - 245760 = 278528. Recursion with lru_cache maybe okay. Iterative maybe faster. Both fine. The user asked "commit-implement-recheck" but final code only. We can choose iterative for performance and no recursion. But recursive is easier to read. Need decide.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sum of the number of moves made by both players until there are no more pawns on the board" Could "moves made by both players" include the moves of the knight during a turn; each turn's move count is the distance. Sum over turns. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "the knight may pass other pawns without capturing them" and "Only the selected pawn can be captured in this turn" Could the knight pass through a pawn and that pawn be considered "not captured" but still block movement
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def maxMoves(self, kx: int, ky: int, positions: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(positions)
        points = positions + [[kx, ky]]
        m = n + 1

        knight_moves = [
            (1, 2), (1, -2), (-1, 2), (-1, -2),
            (2, 1), (2, -1), (-2, 1), (-2, -1)
        ]

        def bfs(sx: int, sy: int):
            dist = [[-1] * 50 for _ in range(50)]
            q = deque([(sx, sy)])
            dist[sx][sy] = 0

            while q:
                x, y = q.popleft()
                for dx, dy in knight_moves:
                    nx, ny = x + dx, y + dy
                    if 0 <= nx < 50 and 0 <= ny < 50 and dist[nx][ny] == -1:
                        dist[nx][ny] = dist[x][y] + 1
                        q.append((nx, ny))

            return dist

        dist = [[0] * m for _ in range(m)]
        for i, (x, y) in enumerate(points):
            d = bfs(x, y)
            for j, (px, py) in enumerate(points):
                dist[i][j] = d[px][py]

        size = 1 << n
        dp = [[0] * m for _ in range(size)]

        for mask in range(1, size):
            remaining = mask.bit_count()
            captured = n - remaining
            alice_turn = (captured % 2 == 0)

            pawns = [i for i in range(n) if mask & (1 << i)]

            for last in range(m):
                if last < n and (mask & (1 << last)):
                    continue

                if alice_turn:
                    best = -10**18
                    for i in pawns:
                        val = dist[last][i] + dp[mask ^ (1 << i)][i]
                        if val > best:
                            best = val
                else:
                    best = 10**18
                    for i in pawns:
                        val = dist[last][i] + dp[mask ^ (1 << i)][i]
                        if val < best:
                            best = val

                dp[mask][last] = best

        return dp[size - 1][n]
```
